If 2. Ke2, the king is safer.
If 1. dxc5, the move `... Qd2+` is still available.
However, the pawn on c5 is gone, so `... c4+` is no longer possible.

Let's compare this to `1. Bd2`.
1. Bd2 attacks the queen.
1... c4+ 2. Ke2 (or Ke4).
Now the queen is still on b4.

1. Bd2 is a tempo gain.

Actually, in many cases, taking the material and simplifying is better.
But let's check the position once more.
White is down a rook and a knight for a bishop.
White is significantly behind in material.
When you are behind in material, you need to create complications or find ways to win material back.
1. dxc5 wins a pawn.
1. Bd2 forces the queen to move.

Let's look at the move `a1c3`.
1. Qc3.
If 1... c4+ 2. Ke2.
Now the queen is on c3, which is a strong square.

Is there any way to trap the Black queen?
She is on b4.
If White plays `d4c5` and then `a1b2`? No.
If White plays `Bd2` and then `Qc3`?
1. Bd2 Qb2+ (not a check) or 1... Qa4.
If 1... Qa4, then 2. Qc3.

The King on d3 is the main weakness.
The pawn on c5 is a threat.
The Queen on b4 is a threat.
Taking the pawn with `d4c5` removes one threat.
Moving the bishop to `d2` attacks the queen and forces her to move.

1. dxc5.

`f1e2` - develops bishop.
`a1c3` - develops queen.
`d3e2` - moves king to safety.

Wait, if I move the king to e2 immediately?
1. Ke2.
Now `... c4` is not a check.
And `... Qd2+` is not possible since the king is on e2. (Wait, e2 to d2 is a queen move).
If 1. Ke2, then 1... Qd2+ is still a check!
Because the Queen is on b4 and the King is on e2.
Wait, b4 to d2 is a move, and d2 to e2 is a move.
No, if the king is on e2, and the queen is on b4, the queen cannot check the king on e2.
b4 to e2: (3, -2). Not a queen move.
So 1. Ke2 removes the threat of `... Qd2+`.
Does it remove the threat of `... c4`?
If 1. Ke2, then 1... c4.
Now the king is on e2. Is c4 a check?
c4 to e2: (2, -2). Yes, it's a diagonal!
So 1... c4 is a check if the king is on e2.

Okay, so both `... Qd2+` and `... c4+` are threats.
Taking the pawn with `d4c5` removes the `... c4+` threat.
Moving the bishop to `d2` attacks the queen and removes the `... Qd2+` threat.

Which is better?
1. dxc5.
1... Qxc5 (Tempting for black).
Now the threat of `... c4+` is gone.
The threat of `... Qd2+` is also gone because the queen is now on c5.

This looks like a very strong sequence.
1. dxc5 Qxc5.
Now White has stabilized the king's position and won a pawn.

Let's check for any other tricks.
Is there any way Black can checkmate or win material?
White's king is on d3. Black has a queen on b4.
If 1. dxc5, the only check is 1... Qd2+.
2. Ke2 or 2. Ke4.

The queen is on d2.
The king is on e2.

- ... Bb7? (No, bishop is on c8).
- ... c4+? (No, pawn was on c5, now it's gone).

So `1. dxc5` followed by `1... Qd2+ 2. Ke2` seems safe.
And `1. dxc5 followed by 1... Qxc5` also seems safe.
